國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國財政壓力仍高，房市降溫與通膨回升增添經濟不確定性 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1,668 億美元，年減 51.6%，主因收入增
加、支出下降，但部分支出提前至7 月支付，影響單月表現。累計前11
個月赤字仍近 2 兆美元，關稅收入轉正，惟利息支出持續攀升，財政壓
力仍高。 
⚫ 8 月美國成屋銷售年率降至 398 萬戶，月減 2.0%、年減 1.2%，為 2025
年 6 月以來首度跌破 400 萬戶，顯示高房貸利率持續抑制房市需求。房
價中位數為 42.91 萬美元，年增 1.6%，連續 38 個月上漲；待售成屋庫
存增至 162 萬戶，相當於 4.9 個月供給量，創逾 10 年高點。整體而言，
成屋市場呈現銷售放緩、房價續升與庫存增加態勢，供給改善有助市場
逐步趨於平衡。 
⚫ 8 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%，高於上月 0.1%，年增率維持
3.4%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，年增率則降至 2.4%。能源價格由跌轉升，
其中汽油上漲 3.9%， 住房月增0.3%， 為推升通膨主因 ； 食品月增0.1%。
整體而言，短期物價壓力升溫，但核心通膨仍持續降溫。 
⚫ 8 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)月增 0.4%、年增 5.4%，高於上月的 0.1%
及 4.8%，主要反映能源價格回升影響，其中柴油價格大漲24.1%；服務
價格則受運輸及倉儲成本增加推升而續呈上揚。核心 PPI 月增 0.3%，
低於前月的 0.4%，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍具韌性，惟基礎通膨動能尚
未明顯升溫。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美加貿易衝突持續升溫 ， 加拿大自9 月 8 日起對美國商品加徵 15%
至 50%報復性關稅 ；美國則擴大限制部分加拿大乳製品 、酒類及機車產
品輸美，並限制加拿大企業參與部分政府採購，雙方爭端已由關稅戰延
伸至市場准入領域，增添北美供應鏈及貿易前景不確定性。 
⚫ 美國國會推進對俄羅斯新制裁法案 ， 擬授權總統對俄羅斯能源主要購買
國商品課徵最高 100%關稅，以壓縮俄方能源收入。法案雖獲參議院高
票支持，但眾議院前景仍不明，且可能波及中國、印度及部分盟友，引
發貿易與外交疑慮。 
短評 ⚫ 美國財政赤字雖因付款時點移轉而在 8 月明顯縮減 ， 但累計赤字仍接近

品依賴國際供應鏈支持。

115.9.14 
一、 美國—財政壓力仍高，房市降溫與通膨回升增添經濟不確定性 
2026 年 8 月(2026 財年第 11 個月)美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1,668.0 億美元，
較去年同期 3,447.9 億美元縮減 51.6%；當月政府收入約 3,600.3 億美元，年增
4.6%，政府支出約 5,268.3 億美元，年減23.5%，惟部分原定 8 月初支付的軍人薪
資與退休金、退伍軍人福利、補充保障收入及部分醫療保險給付款項因 8 月 1 日
適逢非工作日而提前至 7 月支付，使單月支出受到付款時點影響。 收入方面，8
月關稅淨收入約 128.4 億美元，扭轉 7 月關稅淨收入為負 85.5 億美元的情況；累
計前 11 個月關稅淨收入約 1,673.1 億美元，較去年同期增加 1.3%。 支出方面，
8 月淨利息支出約 856.1 億美元，累計前 11 個月達約 1 兆 169.7 億美元，年增
8.9%，顯示利息負擔持續增加。 累計 2026 財年前 11 個月，美國聯邦政府財政
赤字約 1 兆 9,655.9 億美元，較去年同期小幅縮減 0.4%，其中收入年增 3.3%至約
4 兆 8,454.5 億美元，支出年增 2.2%至約 6 兆 8,110.4 億美元。整體而言，8 月單
月赤字雖明顯縮減 ， 但部分受到付款時點移轉影響 ， 且累計赤字仍接近2 兆美元 ，
加上利息支出持續攀升，美國聯邦財政壓力仍維持高檔。 
2026 年 8 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率降至 398 萬戶 ， 較上月減少2.0%，
較去年同期下降 1.2%，並為 2025 年 6 月以來首次跌破 400 萬戶，顯示高房貸利
率持續抑制房市交易動能。區域表現方面，西部銷售持平，東北部、中西部及南
部分別月減 4.0%、3.1%及 1.6%；房價方面，成屋售價中位數為42.91 萬美元，年
增 1.6%，連續 38 個月上漲。供給端則持續改善，待售成屋庫存增至 162 萬戶，
月增 3.2%、年增 5.9%，相當於 4.9 個月供給量，為近 10 多年來最高水準，住房
可負擔指數亦由去年同期的 101.2 升至 104.7。整體而言，8 月美國成屋市場呈現
銷售放緩、房價續漲及庫存增加的格局，高利率仍抑制成交動能，惟供給增加及
可負擔性改善，有助提升購屋者的選擇與議價空間，並促進市場逐步趨於平衡。 
2026 年 8 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，高於上月的
0.1%，年增率維持 3.4%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，高於前月的 0.2%，但年增率由
2.5%降至 2.4%。在細項方面，能源價格由跌轉升，能源指數月增 2.1%，其中汽
油價格上漲 3.9%，成為推升整體物價的主要因素；住房指數月增 0.3%，食品指

數月增 0.1%。此外，通訊、外宿住宿、機票、教育及車輛價格均呈上漲，醫療照
護及汽車保險價格則下跌。整體而言，8 月美國通膨回升主要受汽油與住房價格
上漲帶動，短期物價壓力有所增強；惟核心 CPI 年增率續降，顯示基礎通膨仍維
持降溫趨勢。 
2026 年 8 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，高於上月之
0.1%，年增率則由上月的 4.8%升至 5.4%。其中最終需求商品價格月增 1.1%，結
束前兩個月下跌走勢 ， 超過四分之三的漲幅係由能源價格月增4.2%所帶動 ； 細項
中，柴油價格大漲 24.1%，汽油、航空燃油及燃料油價格亦同步上升，食品價格
則微增 0.1%。另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.1%，已連續第三個月上升，
主要受運輸及倉儲服務價格上漲 2.3%支撐 ， 其中貨車貨運服務及航空客運服務價
格分別上漲 2.0%及 4.2%，惟批發及零售業利潤率下滑，使部分服務價格漲幅受
到抵銷。此外，剔除食品、能源及貿易服務後之核心最終需求PPI 月增 0.3%，低
於 7 月之 0.4%，年增率維持 4.7%。整體而言，8 月 PPI 回升主要反映能源價格反
彈及運輸成本上升影響，顯示美國生產端通膨壓力仍具韌性；惟核心 PPI 增幅較
前月略為放緩，基礎通膨動能尚未進一步升溫，後續仍須持續關注能源價格波動
及服務成本變化對整體物價走勢之影響。 
近期美加貿易衝突持續升溫，雙方關稅報復行動已進一步擴大至非關稅措施
領域 。 加拿大自9 月 8 日起正式對約 200 億美元以上美國商品課徵 15%至 50%不
等的報復性關稅，涵蓋鋼鐵、鋁製品、家電、服飾及農業設備等產品，以回應美
國先前對加拿大商品加徵關稅措施 。 面對加拿大反制 ， 美國政府隨即宣布擴大限
制措施，除關稅措施外，將自 9 月 29 日起限制部分加拿大乳製品、酒類及機車
產品輸美，並限制加拿大企業參與部分美國政府採購標案，使雙方爭端由關稅戰
延伸至市場准入及政府採購領域。 加拿大總理 Mark Carney 表示，美國措施可
能衝擊加拿大關鍵產業發展，加方將持續採取對等回應措施。市場亦關注美方後
續可能進一步提高加拿大汽車、鋼鐵及相關零組件關稅，增添北美供應鏈及企業
投資規劃不確定性。整體而言，美加經貿關係短期內仍難見明顯緩和，後續發展
恐進一步增添北美及全球貿易前景不確定性。 
美國國會近期推進針對俄羅斯的新制裁法案，內容包括授權總統對俄羅斯原
油或天然氣主要購買國的進口商品課徵最高 100%關稅，以進一步限制俄羅斯能
源收入並加大對俄施壓 。 該法案已獲參議院以高票通過 ， 眾議院則預計展開審議 ，

但通過前景仍具不確定性。部分民主黨議員認為，相關條款可能過度擴張總統關
稅裁量權，且中國、印度及部分美國盟友均可能受到影響，引發對貿易關係、消
費成本及外交後果的疑慮，因此後續立法進展及實際執行範圍仍有待觀察。 
二、歐元區—歐洲央行如期升息一碼，且仍有繼續升息的空間 
 歐元區第二季經濟成長季率終值由初值上修 0.2 個百分點至 0.6%，但上修主
因來自愛爾蘭經濟大幅成長 10.2%；若排除愛爾蘭，則歐元區第二季經濟成長季
率僅約 0.3%。從需求面觀察，第二季消費貢獻 0.2 個百分點，雖然通膨上升侵蝕
購買力，但薪資漲幅仍優於預期，加上高儲蓄率支持，使消費動能得以延續。其
中 ， 服務消費維持穩健成長 ， 耐久財支出亦明顯增加 。 另方面 ， 淨出口貢獻0.9 個
百分點，但設備投資下滑使整體固定投資表現仍顯疲弱。 
 歐洲央行 9 月 10 日如期宣布升息一碼，存款機制利率調升為 2.5%，但仍維
持「依經濟數據逐次決策」的立場。值得注意的是，本次歐洲央行分別上修2026
年、2027 年經濟成長率 0.1、0.2 個百分點至 0.9%、1.4%，以及 2028 年核心通膨
上修 0.1 個百分點至 2.3%， 反映歐洲央行認為經濟尚具韌性 ， 即便通膨壓力增溫 ，
仍能減輕其負面影響，使得政策利率繼續上調的空間，故一般預期歐洲央行將會
在 10 月政策會議採取觀望，並在12 月宣布升息一碼，2027 年第一季升息機率亦
有所上升，歐洲央行貨幣政策短期內仍將維持偏鷹立場。 
三、日本—內閣府上修第二季經濟成長，薪資動能續強 
2026 年第二季經濟成長率由 1.1%上修至 1.4%，主要受國內需求及庫存
貢獻改善帶動，其中以資本支出修正為國內需求上修的主因。展望下半年，
在政府財政刺激措施、企業持續擴大設備投資及薪資成長帶動消費支出的支
撐下，日本經濟可望維持溫和成長態勢，惟外部需求復甦力道、能源價格變
動及地緣政治風險仍為影響經濟表現的不確定因素。 
7 月薪資年增 4.7%，優於市場預期，其中夏季獎金年增 6.3%為主要推升
因素；經常性薪資及兼職時薪分別年增 4.1%及 5.7%，增速均較前期擴大 ， 顯
示薪資成長動能持續增強。此外，實質薪資年增3.4%，反映勞工購買力進一
步改善。在企業獲利表現穩健支撐下，儘管仍面臨成本上升及外部環境不確
定性 ， 企業仍維持獎金發放與加薪趨勢 ， 有助支撐民間消費韌性及經濟表現 。

四、中國—進出口同步強勁成長，貿易順差增幅則相對有限 
8 月出口年增率較 7 月回升 1.1 個百分點至 25%，出口總值為 4,014 億美元；
累計前八個月出口年增率為 19.3%，累積出口總值為 29,238 億美元。前八個月
IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)累積出口額分別成長 103.9%、49.4%；船
舶累積出口成長 35.2%；汽車累積出口成長 53.2%。另方面，8 月進口年增率較 7
月回升 0.7 個百分點至 28.2%， 進口總值為2,824 億美元 ； 累計前八個月進口值成
長 28.2%至 21,183 億美元，其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)進口分別
成長 61.7%、105.1%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部分伺服器關鍵組件有賴
於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前八個月貿易順差達 8,055 億美元，僅較去年
同期增加 2.6%， 增幅明顯低於出口 ， 顯示出口擴張同時伴隨關鍵原物料進口大幅
增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價值產品依賴國際供應鏈支
持。 
8 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 7 月的 3.5%回升至 3.8%，主因原油價格自 7 月
起走升，其影響逐漸反映於上游生產成本。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格年
增率反彈 7.3 個百分點至 10.5%，並帶動燃料加工業通膨上揚，抵銷近期鋼鐵價
格下滑，導致相關礦產開採及冶煉加工產品價格較去年同期下滑的影響。此外，
對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學工業、化纖產業通膨沒有明顯變動，部分可能
與先前油價上漲期間累積較高庫存有關，惟在近期油價再度走升下，不排除相關
成本壓力於未來數月逐步反映。另因金價盤整，有色金屬礦採選業出廠價格年增
率亦持續放慢。 
8 月消費者通膨(CPI)回升 0.3 個百分點為 0.8%，但仍然是近七個月的次低水
準，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)回升 0.1 個百分點至 1 %。當月受國際原油價格上
漲影響，燃油通膨反彈 7.5 個百分點至 8.3%；手機零組件上漲，通信工具通膨上
揚 2.3 個百分點至 10.6%；夏季旅遊需求支持旅遊服務通膨增加 0.1 個百分點至
2.7%；其他商品及服務價格則大致平穩。整體而言，8 月能源價格有所回升，但
在企業庫存水位仍偏高情況下，抑制成本的傳導速度，除燃油價格外，尚未明顯
反映於其他消費項目通膨。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，若油價維持高檔或
進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。

1.5%至 3.5%的目標區間內。(2)泰國：8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率上升至

需求及中國供應鏈移轉，帶動電子產品出口，但主要集中於外資企業，資本財

五、台灣—通膨略降溫，AI 需求支撐出口成長 
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.04%，雖較前兩月回落，但已連續 4 個月高
於 2%的通膨警戒線，主要受國際油價上漲帶動油料費、機票及旅遊團費增加，
加上外食費、房租及電費等價格上漲所致；惟蔬菜價格因去年基期較高而大幅下
跌，抵銷部分漲幅。整體而言，通膨壓力雖略有降溫，但核心 CPI 仍達 2.30%，
顯示外食、房租等服務類價格仍具黏著性，物價上漲壓力尚未完全消退。 
 8 月出口達 824 億美元，年增 41%，續創歷年單月新高，主要受惠於人工智
慧應用快速擴展及國際雲端服務業者持續加大資本支出，帶動半導體、伺服器及
相關資通訊產品需求強勁。雖然部分傳統產業仍受全球景氣復甦步調不一及貿易
環境不確定性影響，但科技產品需求熱絡使整體出口表現優於預期。展望未來，
在 AI、 高效能運算及雲端服務需求持續成長 ， 以及第四季傳統外銷旺季來臨帶動
下，我國出口動能可望延續，惟仍須關注地緣政治風險及國際貿易政策變化對外
需之影響。

2408 南亞科(Nanya Technology)

報告日期： 2026/10/13 研 究 員：翁浩軒  (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 毛利率優於預期且將維持 8 成以上 
 2027 年 DRAM 供需仍緊俏 
 新廠 2027 年第四季加入貢獻

一. 3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 25.4 元 
南亞科 3Q26 營收 1336.49 億(QoQ+61.9% 、YoY+611.7%) ，其中位元
出貨量季減 4~6% ， 平均銷售單價季增超過 60%(主要因反應長期合
約重新簽訂之調整)，匯率影響有利 1~3%，3Q26 毛利率上升至
85.7%，主要因平均銷售單價上升，營業費用季增 28 億元，因當
期及未來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利 876.27 億(QoQ+74.58%、
YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

二. 4Q26 整體產業仍將維持供不應求態勢  
受惠於 AI 需求 ， AI 運算（GPU 、 TPU 、 CPU 及 ASIC）成為 DRAM 
需求成長主力，帶動高容量、高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求，
範疇從 HBM 、LPDDR5 進一步擴展至網路、儲存與系統管理等領
域。在傳統應用市場方面，由於 DRAM 供給不足與 ASP 上漲，
部分傳統應用終端出現產品降規或延長生命週期的現象，低階市
場則有回收 DRAM 支撐部分需求。供給端方面，因新廠建置、設
備交期、製程導入均需要較長的前置時間，目前供給仍維持緊俏，
智慧型手機、個人電腦及一般消費性記憶體供給受限，製造商與
供應鏈端的庫存水位普遍偏低 ， 隨著供應商依 LTA 長約進行產品
組合與產能規劃，有利未來供需穩定度。

三. 新廠預估 4Q27 開始投產 
公司 5A 新廠預計 2027 年第一季裝機，2027 年第四季開始投
產 ， 預計 2028 年底前達到每月投片量 3 萬片 ， 全產能目標於 2030 
年底前達每月 5 萬片，未來產能擴充均以客戶長期合約的 AI 專
案為主要目標。另外公司亦佈局先進封測領域，規劃於屏東科學
園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠 ， 預估 2H28 設備進駐 。 公司 2026
年前三季累計資本支出為 276 億元 ， 2026 年資本支出以不超過 697
億元為上限，其中生產設備資本支出約占五成。

四.  2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 77.1 元/ 134.3 元

展望 2026 下半年至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大 ， 接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 ， 小型
語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記
憶體需求持續上揚。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅增加產能，預估
2027 年新增產能有限，多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新
增產能實際貢獻要到 2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科新廠預計 2028 年放
量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，全產能將達 5 萬片/月。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 670 元 
南亞科為全球前五大 DRAM 廠，受惠於 AI 帶動之供給端改善，記
憶體產品漲價快速，南亞科今明年獲利創高，2028 年全球雖然有
新產能開出，但隨著 AI 由模型訓練與推理延伸至更多客製化邊緣
AI 應用，帶動記憶體位元需求長期成長。考量到 AI 應用滲透率提
升帶動記憶體需求，記憶體已成戰略物資，客戶亦開始簽訂長約，
參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 5 倍本益比，目標價 670 元，建議買進

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
南亞科成立於 1 995 年，為 DRAM （動態隨機存取記憶體）之研
發、設計、製造與銷售商，為全球第四大 DRAM 廠，市佔率約 
3%，由美光授權製程技術，其十二吋晶圓廠位於泰山南林園區，
月產能 6.8 萬片 ， 其中 20nm 製程約 3.8 萬片 ， 30nm 製程約 3 萬
片，公司產品廣泛應用於手機、PC、消費性電子產品，主要競爭
對手包括 SK Hynix、三星電子、美光、華邦電等。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. AI 帶動記憶體價格雙位數上漲 
全球 CSP 大廠持續加強投資 AI server 及相關基礎建設，全球九大
CSP 廠之資本支出持續高速成長，2026 年資本支出將達 9220 億元
(YoY+98%)，2027 年資本支出成長至 13830 億美元(YoY+50%)。
TrendForce 預估 2026 年 HBM 需求量年增 70%。而隨著 AI 應用由
LLM 模型訓練延伸至推論，CSP 廠擴大通用型伺服器布建，也使
記憶體拉貨重心由 HBM 延伸至 RDIMM ， 進一步推升 DDR5 價格。
3Q26 在 AI 與資料中心建置需求持續下記憶體仍嚴重供不應求 ， 儘
管價格基期已高，但預估 DRAM 合約價持續上漲，TrendForce 預
估第三季整體 conventional DRAM 合約價上漲 13-18% ， 第四季持續
上漲 10%~15%。供給方面，DRAM 三大原廠今年新增產能有限，
三星及海力士均以既有廠區調整及製程轉換為主，預估晶圓產能
分別增加 5%及 8%，而美光產能持平，三大原廠新廠產能開出並

公司研究報告

證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

3 
明顯貢獻預估在 2028 年 。 預估 2026 年 DRAM 價格逐季調升 ， 2027
年新增供給有限下，價格易漲難跌。

2. 2027 年記憶體需求維持強勁 
展望 2026 第四季至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大，接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 (如: 
Edge、客製化)，小型語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置 (PC、手機、汽
車、機器人等)，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記憶體需求持續
上揚。需求面依各應用別而言，Server 方面，AI 及高效能運算持
續驅動 HBM 與 DDR5、LPDDR5 需求，高階 e-SSD、BMC、NIC
對 DDR4、LPDDR4 及 DDR5 需求顯著; PC 及手機方面，高階 PC
與手機對 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 需求維持穩定 ， 然中低階需求疲弱; 消
費型電子市場方面，AI 及一般伺服器對 HBM 、RDIMM 需求持續
擴張，排擠一般消費型記憶體供給。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅
增加產能，聚焦於高階雲端與高附加價值產品需求，DRAM 大廠
優先分配產能於 DDR5 及 HBM 產品 ， 預估 2027 年新增產能有限，
多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新增產能實際貢獻要到
2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科第二代 10 奈米級製程(1B) 16Gb DDR5 產
品交運量已達 10%，第三代 10 奈米級(1C)及第四代 10 奈米級(1D)
製程技術試產中。公司 DDR5 營收約占 10%，並可視需求彈性調
整增加，同時持續供應 DDR4 及 LPDDR4 產品以緩解市場缺口。
公司亦積極與策略夥伴合作拓展 AI 邊緣運算驅動的記憶體市場，
目前處工程驗證階段，預計於 2026 年底推出新產品。南亞科 2025
年資本支出 134 億元，2026 年資本支出預計不超過 697 億元，其
中生產設備支出約占五成，新廠預計於 2027 年第一季開始裝機，
2027 年第四季開始生產，2028 年放量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，
預估 2030 年全產能將達 5 萬片/月。南亞科 2025 年位元銷售量成
長 50 多%，預估 2026 年成長 17~19%，預估 2027 年成長中低個位
數。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

1、3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 25.4 元 
南亞科 3Q26 營收 1336.49 億(QoQ+61.9% 、YoY+611.7%) ，其中位元
出貨量季減 4~6% ， 平均銷售單價季增超過 60%(主要因反應長期合
約重新簽訂之調整)，匯率影響有利 1~3%，3Q26 毛利率上升至
85.7%，主要因平均銷售單價上升，營業費用季增 28 億元，因當
期及未來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利 876.27 億(QoQ+74.58%、
YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

南亞科 (2408 TT)  Buy

獲利處於上行，建議 Buy。  
投資建議 ： AI 運算 (GPU 、 TPU、 CPU 及 ASIC) 主導整體 DRAM
需求強勁成長，全球記憶體產業景氣循環邁入長期結構性擴張。
AI 應用領域持續擴大，推升更高容量、更高頻寬及客製化記憶
體架構需求，需求已由核心之 HBM 與 LPDDR5 全面擴展至網
路、儲存及系統管理等應用，而供應商將產能部署於 HBM 與先
進製程，對常規 DRAM 造成實質產能排擠。南亞科目前簽署長
期供應協議 (LTA) 的產能約占總出貨量的 50~70% 。不過南亞科
位元出貨量已達高點，現有廠區在 4Q27 新廠量產前產能維持
滿載，出貨量維持平穩走勢，加上公司庫存處於低檔，預估南
亞科 2027 年 bit shipment 持平，預估南亞科 2027 年稅後 EPS 
127.64 元。主要 DRAM 大廠將產能重心轉向 HBM 與 DDR5 等
高階產品，壓縮 DDR4 供給，使南亞科逐步成為 DDR4 的最大
供應商，議價能力持續提升，建議南亞科 Buy，目標價 635 元。 
3Q26 稅後 EPS 25.40 元：受惠 AI 基礎設施拉貨與長約效益浮
現，3Q26 營收為 1,336.49 億元，QoQ+61.90% ，ASP QoQ>+60% 。
匯率對營收影響 QoQ+1~3% ， bit shipment QoQ-4~-6% 。毛利率
受 Blend ASP 上升， 3Q26 毛利率上揚至 85.66% ，增加 6.17 個
百分點。南亞科 3Q26 稅後 EPS 25.40 元，淨值 118.99 元。  
預計 2026 年資本支出 697 億元 ：南亞科董事會決議為加速 5A
新廠建置並提前支付設備預付款，將 2026 年資本支出預算由原
訂的新台幣 520 億元，大幅上調至不超過新台幣 697 億元，增
加 177 億元，增幅約 34% ，較 2025 年資本支出新台幣 134 億
元，YoY 增加 420.15% ，資本支出調升主要係於 5A 新廠建置與
EUV 先進設備預付款。資本支出中 50% 用在新廠的廠務和無塵
室等，其餘 50% 則會使用在設備採購上。 5A 新廠第一階段月產
能規劃為 35.9K 片，預計於 1Q27 進行設備移入裝機， 4Q27 開
始投產貢獻出貨，目標於 2028 年底前達成每月投片量 30K 片，
並於 2030 年底前達成全產能每月投片量 50K 片之長期目標。

2 群益投顧 
  南亞科 ESG ：  
佈局綠色科技生產  
DRAM 為智慧世代的關鍵零組件，南亞科研發先進且具高效能的 「環境友善
產品」以符合市場與客戶需求。所有產品於設計初期時，即導入生命週期思
維與綠色設計，透過綠色產品推動委員會進行綠色產品管理。

重視人才發展與培養  
「人才」是公司創新、營運與永續發展的根本，強化公司全方位人才培育與
發展，南亞科技與長庚大學簽署成立記憶體學程碩士班，學生除可獲高額獎
學金，更可獲得畢業後的就業機會。南亞科技期盼從育才、用才、引才、聚
才、留才各方面，強化臺灣記憶體產業在國際上的競爭力。

共同攜手永續夥伴  
南亞科攜手上下游合作夥伴一同前行， 295 家供應商認同公司的永續理念，
一同簽署南亞科技企業社會責任承諾書且簽署率達 100% 、輔導供應商進行
永續改善，稽核、輔導與改善率均 100% 完成，並持續辦理供應鏈研討會。
此外，也與供應商共同合作推動人權及環境相關的議題。

永續績效備受肯定  
南亞科視 ESG 為企業的重要承諾，南亞科追求經營績效的同時，也同步落
實 ESG 的平衡發展。南亞科技參加 2025 第 18 屆台灣企業永續獎評選，公
司已連續第九年獲頒多項殊榮，獲獎包括年度最佳報告 (製造業 )、台灣 100
大永續典範企業獎及四項永續單項績效  – 氣候領袖獎、創新成長領袖獎、
資訊安全領袖獎與透明誠信領袖獎。  
南亞科已連續第五年入選 DJSI 世界指數，並連續第七年入選 S&P 全球永續
年鑑，持續獲得國際永續評鑑機構肯定。  
ASP 大幅增加，南亞科 3Q26 稅後 EPS 25.40 元，淨值 118.99 元：  
受惠長期合約 (LTA) 重新簽訂帶動 ASP 跳升與 AI 運算拉貨動能增溫，南亞
科 3Q26 營業收入達新台幣 1,336.49 億元， QoQ+61.90% ， YoY+611.70% 。
受長期合約重新議價影響，平均銷售單價 (ASP)QoQ>+60% ，YoY>500% ，bit 
shipment( 位元出貨量 )則因產能受限而 QoQ-4~-6% ，匯率變動則產生 1~3%
之有利影響。  
長約到期的重新簽約為近期單價上漲的主要動力 。 3Q26 平均售價 (ASP) 季增
超過 60% ，其中超過一半的漲幅來自於半年前至一年前簽訂的高涵蓋率長
約到期並重新定價。  
終端應用結構方面， AI 基礎設施 (含伺服器 )營收貢獻顯著攀升，佔比已正式
突破 20%，其餘出貨則涵蓋儲存 SSD、網通、車用工控與消費性電子等多元

南亞科董事會決議將 2026 年資本支出預算由原訂的新台幣 520 億元，提高
至不超過新台幣 697 億元：

將 2026 年資本支出預算由原訂的新台幣 520 億元，大幅上調至不超過新台
幣 697 億元，增加 177 億元，增幅約 34% ，較 2025 年資本支出新台幣 134
億元， YoY 增加 420.15% ，資本支出調升主要係於 5A 新廠建置與 EUV 先
進設備預付款。  
資本支出中 50% 用在新廠的廠務和無塵室等，其餘 50% 則會使用在設備採
購上。累計 1Q26 至 3Q26 資本支出已執行新台幣 276 億元。預估 2026 年折
舊費用將較 2025 年減少 10% 左右，等到新廠 2027 年量產後，折舊費用才
會再增加。在位元出貨方面，現有廠區在 4Q27 新廠量產前產能維持滿載，
出貨量維持平穩走勢，加上公司庫存處於低檔，預估南亞科 2026 年 bit 
shipment 約 YoY+17~19% 。

2029 年 4 年不超過 3,466 億元的資本支出上限，全力建設 5A 新廠， 5A 新
廠全期規劃月產能達 50K 片，將導入 10nm(1B-1E) 先進製程及 EUV 技術。

4 群益投顧 
5A 新廠第一階段月產能規劃為 35.9K 片，預計於 1Q27 進行設備移入裝機，
4Q27 開始投產貢獻出貨，目標於 2028 年底前達成每月投片量 30K 片，並
於 2030 年底前達成全產能每月投片量 50K 片之長期目標。  
此外，公司已規劃於屏東科學園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠，全面支援先
進記憶體架構封裝需求。屏東廠長期總資本支出估計高達 100 億美元，預計
於 2H28 開始進行設備移入與裝機。南亞科 (前段晶圓級 )主導前段的晶圓級
處理 (Wafer-level processing) 與晶圓級測試 (Wafer-level testing) 。由合作夥伴
福懋科負責後續的晶圓研磨、切割、打線、晶片封裝與最終測試。  
受惠技術自主研發與客製化合作深化，南亞科藉由靈活之產能配置積極應
對市場規格演進。產品布局上維持高度多樣化策略，同時供貨 DDR5 、
LPDDR5/5X 、DDR4、LPDDR4/4X 及 DDR3 系列，針對常規應用與利基型規
格動態調配投片比例，並透過 LTA 長約機制鎖定產能與合理利潤，有效抵
禦成熟規格現貨報價波動之潛在風險。  
1B/1C/1D 和 EUV 進度  
南亞科的 10nm 製程分為 A、 B、 C、 D、 E 世代，其記憶儲存細胞 (Cell) 可進
行微縮，因此可由 1A 逐步推進至 1B 再到 1C。製程轉換照計劃進行，會視
整體景氣動態調整。 1B 製程已滿載且良率高， 2026 年產出目標占比達 40%。
1C 製程預計於 1Q27 正式交付客戶進行驗證，次世代 1D、1E 製程及極紫外
光 (EUV) 微影技術導入開發進度亦完全符合內部規劃。現有廠區在既有空間
內持續優化先進節點投片轉換，以確保 DDR5 與 LPDDR5 等高階規格產出
持續攀升；  
EUV 在 1C 和 1D 製程都有導入，甚至 1E，還是要看客戶需求，目前預期
2028 年導入採用；在興建新廠時亦會同步建置 EUV 極紫外光微影設備獨立
廠房。 1C/1D/1E 及 EUV 研發也依計畫推進。  
4Q26 成長來自價格向上，預估南亞科 4Q26 稅後 EPS 28.94 元：  
DRAM 是算力的關鍵零組件，在 AI 驅動的投資下，推動記憶體供需結構性
改變，降低景氣循環因素。 HBM、LPDDR5 與 RDIMM 需求強勁。 HBM、高
容量 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 是整體 DRAM 需求成長的主要動能，高階 e-SSD 、
BMC 、 NIC 對 DDR4 、 LPDDR4 及 DDR5 需求持續。  
一般型 DRAM 方面，高階 PC 與手機對 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 需求維持穩定；
其他消費性方面，高價消費型產品需求暢旺，低價產品需求放緩，整體而言，
AI 需求強排擠手機、 PC、車用及消費型記憶體供給，預計記憶體供給不足
預期將持續數季以上，長期 LTA ( 數年合約 )形成供需共識。  
AI 伺服器與高效能運算拉貨動能外溢，加上手機與一般運算記憶體供給端
受限， 4Q26 DRAM 產業結構性缺貨態勢維持不變。原廠庫存普遍偏低，且
供應商均依據 LTA 長約承諾進行排產，新廠建置、設備交期與新製程導入
均需要較長前置時間，整體供給彈性極為有限，供需緊俏格局為 4Q26 營運
提供基本面支撐。 4Q26 報價已確定高於 3Q26 ，南亞科多數產品合約價持續
上揚，公司對整體價格走勢樂觀，且長約占比持續提高，短單價格仍可能因
市況波動而調整。南亞科 2H26 成長主要來自 ASP，因為庫存在低點，產能
利用率已滿載，預估南亞科 4Q26 稅後 EPS 28.94 元。預估 2026 年稅後 EPS

### Page 5

個股報告

5 群益投顧 
76.44 元。  
產能受限，預估南亞科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.64 元：  
AI 運算 (GPU 、 TPU、 CPU 及 ASIC) 主導整體 DRAM 需求強勁成長，全球記
憶體產業景氣循環邁入長期結構性擴張。 AI 應用領域持續擴大，推升更高
容量 、 更高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求 ， 需求已由核心之HBM 與 LPDDR5
全面擴展至網路、儲存及系統管理等應用，而供應商將產能部署於 HBM 與
先進製程，對常規 DRAM 造成實質產能排擠，使 DRAM 供需缺口將長期持
續 ， 關鍵觀察指標為北美CSP 及大型語言模型開發商的 AI 投資回報率 (ROI)
是否可持續。  
傳統應用市場在供給不足與價格調漲環境下，傳統應用產品出現規格下調
(down-specification) 或延長生命週期的狀況，低階市場部分由二手或回收
DRAM 支撐。  
DDR4 走勢：  
 供給短缺：由於主要供應商減產並將產能轉移至  HBM/DDR5 ，DDR4 市
場出現供給短缺，且此狀況預計將持續數個季度。  
 市場轉移至 DDR5 ：雖然 DDR4 市場占比逐漸變小，目前單一 DDR4 產
品仍佔整體 DRAM 市場總需求 12%~14% ， 若加計 LPDDR4 則比例更高 。
南亞科全球 DRAM 市佔率約為 3%，因此相對龐大的 DDR4 需求，為公
司提供相當充沛且健康的銷售空間。  
 需求穩固：消費性電子是 DDR4 需求最穩固的領域。 8Gb 容量是目前市
場的最佳選擇點 (sweet spot) 。每個客戶的應用中，可能有完整的基礎設
施 (平台 )，包括 PCB、晶片組等等。所以，短期對客戶來說，從 DDR4 轉
移到  DDR5 相對複雜，客戶必須在基礎設施變更上花費大量的時間和金
錢，且 16Gb DDR5 價格也上漲，成本高昂，因此需求將維持較長時間。
 南亞科 16Gb DDR4 於 2H26 開始貢獻營收。南亞科具備高度彈性，可隨
客戶需求與市場效益，在 DDR4 、 LPDDR4 、 DDR5 與 AI 專案之間彈性
調配產能。   
DDR5 ：  
 AI 運算 (GPU、TPU、CPU、ASIC) 全面驅動高容量與高頻寬記憶體需求，
加上個人電腦 (PC) 領域主要採用 DDR5 ，帶動 DDR5 需求穩健成長。  
 南亞科進度：公司已開始以模組形式出貨 DDR5 ，技術進度與市場同步，
高階的 6400 Mbps 產品已準備就緒。  
 目前 DDR5 交易量約占 10% ，公司目標是維持在 10% 以上並逐步提升，
但仍需視整體缺貨結構而定，短期內不會過度拉升產出，以避免對客戶
造成調整壓力。 DDR5 8Gb R-DIMM 主要應用於雲端與伺服器，但在既
有產能未擴增的情況下，大量出貨仍需等待新廠投產後才有空間。

Wafer-on-Wafer (WoW) 堆疊技術：

### Page 6

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 13 日

6 群益投顧 
 公司正積極開發  WoW 技術，以應對未來  AI/AGI 應用所需的高頻寬與
高密度需求。  
 目前相關設備已安裝，並正與多家客戶合作開發不同領域的終端產品，
但因保密協議無法透露具體應用。預估 2027 年有營收貢獻。  
 南亞科正與多位客戶合作開發客製化  DRAM ，其中也包含  HBM 的延
伸產品以及  Wafer-to-Wafer 封裝專案。由於具體進度取決於客戶端的
市場開發時程，目前尚無法給出明確的營收貢獻指引  
 台灣擁有領先晶圓代工廠 (如台積電 )，這的確有助於簡化並推動南亞科
在客製化專案 (如  HBM 及  Wafer-to-Wafer) 上的進行。  
南亞科的長期合約 (LTA) 佔總出貨量的涵蓋率約落在 50%~70% 。 合約期限從
單季到 3 年不等，其中 3 年期的超長約已佔出貨量  20% 以上。隨著 AI 與伺
服器客戶積極爭取長期產能保障，南亞科正持續與客戶推進更長天期的供
應合約，計畫逐步將過去短天期的 1 年期合約，往 3 年以上的「長期供應合
約」進行結構性轉換，以鎖定長期合作夥伴的份額。為應對記憶體產業的週
期性波動，南亞科正與客戶洽談導入包含 「價格底線與上限」 的合約機制 (如
保證採購、價格底線或預付款 )，在保障供應商合理利潤的同時，也為客戶
鎖定成本上限。這些長期合約不僅涵蓋現有的廠房產能，也涵蓋未來新廠的
整體產能規劃。一般來說，如果是季度合約，價格會在一個季度內維持固定，
雙方都會遵守約定，並在下一次談判週期到來前盡量不作調整。  
產業結構性轉變擴大需求缺口，加速多元創新  AI 解決方案，記憶體需求
維持熱絡， AI 相關需求降低景氣循環波動， AI GPU /CPU/ASIC 晶片開發持
續推升 HBM 、 LPDDR5 及 DDR5 需求大幅成長。 AI 基礎建設帶動  eSSD 、
SmartNIC 與 BMC 採用更高容量、更高效 DRAM ，以因應高速資料處理、網
路傳輸及系統管理需求。非 AI 應用產能受限，有利於高階市場發展，使終
端產品售價提升。  
南亞科預期供需緊缺的狀況預計將持續延續至 2027 年甚至 2028 年初。南
亞科目前的策略則是技術自主、長約鎖定與利潤導向的防禦性資本支出，而
非衝產量、殺價拼市占。南亞科目前簽署長期供應協議 (LTA) 的產能約占總
出貨量的 50~70% 。不過南亞科位元出貨量已達高點，現有廠區在 4Q27 新
廠量產前產能維持滿載，出貨量維持平穩走勢，加上公司庫存處於低檔，預
估南亞科 2027 年 bit shipment 持平，價格走勢維持高檔，使南亞科毛利率
水準有望優於 2026 年，預估南亞科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.64 元。

10 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Largan Precision  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 Results Miss on Lower
GPM, but FAU/CPO
Development Well on Track
Largan reported 3Q26 net income of NT$5.182bn, down 27% YoY.
The company reported a 11% YoY revenue decline, to NT$15.651bn; gross margin
declined YoY and QoQ to 42.2% in 3Q26, lower than expected due to lower new-
project yields and structural green electricity cost hikes.
Management expects shipments to be flat MoM in October and decline

sequentially in November as order momentum is weaker-than-expected.
FAU/CPO development well on track: First FA  project passed customer
verification; expanding from FA into FAU, including coupling and potential FAU-to-
carrier assembly, with a dedicated new facility expected to support mass production
from around mid-2027. We maintain our long-term positive view on Largan's FAU/
CPO opportunities.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Largan Precision (3008.TW)
Base case: Our price target is based on an SOTP valuation, comprising the traditional business
and potential FAU opportunity. We value the traditional business using a residual income
model, consistent with our Greater China Hardware Tech coverage, assuming an 8.5% cost of
equity, a 7% net profit CAGR over 2026-36E and a 3% terminal growth rate. We separately

value the FAU business, assuming a 40% market share in 2028.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected high-end smartphone demand
n Faster-than-expected penetration of periscopes
n Stronger-than-expected CPO volume growth
Risks to Downside
n Greater-than-expected competition, which would erode Largan's ASP and gross margin
n Weaker-than-expected smartphone demand, especially for high-end models
n Market share gains and more pricing pressure from Sunny

n Weaker-than-expected CPO volume growth

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響 ， 加上新產
品良率及綠電採購成本壓力 ， 預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對
保守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司
發揮精密光學設計與製造優勢；另一方面， FAU 預計於 2H27
進入量產，隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來
源。 考量大立光具備高精度光學製造、 微米級對位技術及自動化
設備開發能力， 技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢， 正面看待公
司中長期發展。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入
為 706.92 億元，Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，
營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，
Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。研究部考量儘管 2026 年消費
性電子需求疲弱，且 CPO 產品短期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度
優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，2027 年營運有望重回成長
軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目標價訂為 7,800 元，投
資建議為買進。  
⚫ 公司基本資料 
⚫ Largan 大立光電(3008.TT)公司簡介 
大立光電(3008.TT)成立於 1987 年，並於 2002 年台灣證券交易所
掛牌上市。大立光作為全球光學塑膠鏡頭領導廠商，主要業務內容
為研發與製造精密光學塑膠鏡頭，產品主要應用於智慧型手機，其
餘也包含平板、筆記型電腦、車用及其他消費性電子產品，在全球
光學鏡頭市占率約佔 30%。其總部位於台中市南屯區精密機械園
區，共有十座廠區及兩座興建中廠區。前十大股東持股合計
38.06%。客戶為 APPLE、華為、三星、小米、OPPO、VIVO 等手
機品牌廠，主要競爭者則為玉晶光 (3406.TT) 及舜宇光學
(2382.HK)。3Q26 出貨量佔比：20MP 以上約 10~20%、10MP 約

⚫ 產業概況 
記憶體成本飆升壓抑出貨，智慧型手機市場進入量縮價增 
根據研調機構數據 ，2026 年全球智慧型手機市場受記憶體供應短缺
及成本大幅上漲影響 ， 出貨量預估將Y-16.7%至 10.5 億支 ， 較前一
季預估的 Y-13.9%再度下修，並創下歷年最大年度衰退幅度，其中
2H26 出貨量更預估 Y-27.2%。 由於記憶體成本年增超過300%，品
牌廠難以自行吸收成本，陸續透過漲價及調整產品組合因應，帶動
2026 年智慧型手機平均售價 Y+27.6%至 581 美元，全球市場產值
反而 Y+6.3%至 6,130 億美元，呈現「量縮價增」格局。產品結構
方面，低階市場受到衝擊最為明顯，尤其100 美元以下機種因原先
毛利空間有限，品牌廠陸續縮減產品線，2Q26 該價位出貨量已年
減近 60%；相較之下，高階機種因消費者價格敏感度較低，且成熟
市場具分期付款等機制支撐，需求相對具韌性，帶動整體市場加速
朝高階化發展。 
展望未來，記憶體供需緊張預期至少延續至 2028 年，全球市場短

主要受惠新款 iPhone 進入備貨旺季，帶動高階鏡頭出貨增加。毛

良率較低，以及綠電採購成本增加影響。業外收益 13.57 億元，其
中包含匯兌收益 1.97 億元，支撐整體稅後獲利季增。綜合以上，大

⚫ 未來展望 
拉貨動能逐步趨緩，良率改善有望緩解毛利率壓力 
展望 4Q26，預期 10 月拉貨量與 9 月持平，11 月起則隨新機拉貨
高峰結束而逐步下滑 ， 儘管客戶1H27 新產品已自 10 月開始拉貨，
仍難以完全彌補既有訂單下修影響，加上目前客戶需求能見度偏
短，第四季營收動能仍具不確定性。毛利率方面，儘管新機生產良
率逐步改善 ， 然而綠電採購成本持續增加 ， 相關成本最快須至2028
年才能反映於報價，預期短期毛利率改善幅度仍將受到限制。綜合
以上 ， 研究部預估大立光4Q26 營業收入為 155.21 億元 ，Y-9.86%、
Q-0.83%，毛利率 46.65%，Y-1.32ppts、Q+4.44ppts，營業利益
率 35.29%，Y-1.46ppts、Q+5.04ppts，稅後純益 54.91 億元，
Y-18.28%，EPS 為 41.98 元。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2026
年營業收入為 603.80 億元 ，Y-1.26%， 毛利率46.84%，Y-3.59ppts，
營 業 利 益 率 35.06% ，Y-3.47ppts ， 稅 後 純 益 214.67 億元，
Y+0.90%，EPS 為 164.12 元。

手機主鏡頭規格持續升級，高階光學產品支撐營運動能 
展望 2027 年，智慧型手機主鏡頭將有規格升級，帶動高階鏡頭設
計提升，目前相關產品已完成技術準備並持續送樣，有望隨新機導
入逐步貢獻營收。潛望式鏡頭方面，下一波較大幅度規格升級預計
落在 2028 年，目前已進入試產階段，2027 年則以既有產品出貨為
主。獲利方面，因綠電採購成本持續增加，短期毛利率改善仍將取
決於新產品良率及產品組合變化。

FA 預計 2H27 量產，精密對位與整合能力開啟光通訊新成長動能 
光通方面，FA 產品已通過客戶認證，FAU 則持續進行送樣及整合
驗證 ，FAU 預計 2027 年年中完成產線建置 ，2H27 正式進入量產。
初期以單排 FA 及 Grating Couplin 技術為主，並朝 FAU 與 Carrier
整合出貨發展 ， 提高單一產品附加價值 ， 多排FA 及 Edge Coupling
則持續開發 ， 預計2028 年後逐步導入 。 公司憑藉微米級精密對位、
自製自動化設備及檢測能力 ， 可望在高技術門檻的FAU 市場建立競
爭優勢。預期 2027 年光通營收貢獻仍處於起步階段，隨後續產能
與良率提升，有望成為中長期成長動能。綜合以上，研究部預估大
立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元 ，Y+17.08%， 毛利率51.37%，
Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24
億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。

⚫ 結論與建議 
大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響，加上新產品
良率及綠電採購成本壓力，預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對保
守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司發揮
精密光學設計與製造優勢 ； 另一方面 ，FAU 預計於 2H27 進入量產 ，
隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來源。考量大立
光具備高精度光學製造、微米級對位技術及自動化設備開發能力，
技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢，正面看待公司中長期發展。綜
合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元，
Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，
Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08
元。研究部考量儘管2026 年消費性電子需求疲弱 ，且CPO 產品短
期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，
2027 年營運有望重回成長軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目
標價訂為 7,800 元，投資建議為買進。

法国爆发全国性暴⼒抗议，全国逾 55 万⼈上街，多地市政厅及商铺遭毁焚， 2397 ⼈被捕，
受损⾦额达数亿欧元。暴动由学⽣抗议蔓延⾄⻩背⼼与农⺠，核⼼⽭盾在于公共债务占
GDP 达 119% 带来的⾼通胀与财政紧缩。受中东冲突与债市承压影响，法国国债收益率飙
升，⾯临严重的政治与财政双重危机。

同⼀时间，巴黎共和国⼴场上的汽⻋还在烧。⻢赛有⼈⽤隔离护栏撞开市政厅⼤⻔，在墙上喷
了⼀个⽆政府主义的 "A" ，⼈群⾥有⼈⾼喊 " 我们攻占巴⼠底了 " 。⾥昂的旅游局⽼楼被点着，波

### Page 2

波尔多、⾥尔、图卢兹、斯特拉斯堡，超市、药店、眼镜店、⼿机店，还有爱⻢仕和路易威登
的橱窗，⼀个接⼀个被砸开。巴黎市⻓格雷⽠尔说，⾸都 " 整条整条的街道被毁了 " 。
内政部事后给出的数字是：全国 55 万⼈上街，巴黎 11 万。事前，他们预计最多 17 万⼈。
法国总⼯会的说法是 80 万⼈、 1750 个集会点。截⾄ 10 ⽉ 18 ⽇， 2397 ⼈被捕， 997 名警察、宪兵
和消防员受伤，其中 7 ⼈重伤。法国零售与贸易联合会统计，约 1500 家商⼾受损，损失以亿欧元
计。⼀位 78 岁的巴黎居⺠站在街垒残骸旁对法新社说： " 感觉我们像打了⼀场仗。 "
如果只把这些画⾯当成 " 法国⼈⼜上街了 " ，会错过此暴动真正的分量。街上其实挤着五拨互不相
识的⼈：⾼中⽣、⼤学⽣、⼯会成员、农⺠，以及⼋年前那场 " ⻩背⼼ " 运动的⽼⾯孔。他们喊的
⼝号不⼀样，要的东西也不⼀样。
把这五拨⼈黏在⼀起的，是⼀个数字： 119% 。
⼀把⽕从⾼中教室烧到市政厅，只⽤了三个星期
故事起点在巴黎郊区的克雷泰伊。9 ⽉中旬，⼀所⾼中的学⽣最先⾛出校⻔，理由是校舍年久失

修、班级太挤、⽼师不够。抗议随后蔓延到波尔多、雷恩、南特、⾥昂、⾥尔、⻢赛，约 25 万
学⽣卷⼊， 500 多所⾼中停课。
政府的反应不算慢：涨注册费的计划撤了， 3000 名代课教师派下去了，还开了⼀个全国线上问
卷，专⻔听学⽣说话。为平息事态，警⽅⼀度暂停在校园抗议中使⽤震爆弹。
问题在于，法国 2026 年教育部的预算是 645 亿欧元，听着不少，可其中约 285 亿要拿去付退休教
师的养⽼⾦。钱在账上，进不了教室。
在贫困社区教法语的罗歇 - 拉康打了个⽐⽅：⽔池在漏⽔，该修的是池⼦，光往⾥加⽔没⽤。
学⽣更焦虑的，是漏⽔的天花板之外的东西：那张⽂凭还能不能换来⼀份稳定的⼯作。这个焦
虑， 3000 名代课教师回答不了。

于是 " ⻩背⼼ " 回来了。这个 2018 年因为燃油税上街的运动，⼋年之后重新出现在队伍⾥。它的代
表⼈物罗德⾥格斯在抗议前发了⼀段视频： " ⾼中⽣在为他们的未来⽽战，⼯⼈在为靠劳动⽣活
⽽战，退休者在为尊严⽽战。我们的路不同，但我们的担忧可以汇合。 "
10 ⽉ 17 ⽇，戴着⻩⾊背⼼的⾯具抗议者在巴黎共和国⼴场附近。图源：路透社
农⺠的加⼊更直接。中东战事推⾼柴油和化肥价格，夏天⼜赶上极端天⽓，法国农业部估计约 3
万家农场急需援助。拖拉机开进城，和⾼中⽣站在了同⼀条街上。
爱丁堡⼤学当代史教授沙巴尔对半岛电视台说，这是 " ⼀系列抗议的逻辑结果 " ，⽽且把学⽣的怨
⽓和全社会的怨⽓合到了⼀起。对法国政府来说， " 这是最坏的结果 " 。
对抗也在升级。⾃ 9 ⽉中旬以来，超过 6700 名未成年⼈被警⽅拘留。在北部朗斯，⼀枚震爆弹在
学校外爆炸， 15 岁的少年失去了⼀只⼿；在西部图尔， 16 岁的学⽣被橡㬵⼦弹击中，失明；在
圣艾蒂安， 17 岁男孩的右⼿被防暴雷炸断。
这些画⾯在社交平台上反复播放，然后被复制。到 10 ⽉ 17 ⽇，原本 " 改善教学条件 " 的诉求，已经

变成了墙上的 " 向富⼈征税 "" 吃掉富⼈ " ，和街头的 " ⻢克⻰辞职 " 。内政部⻓努内斯在校园抗议中
暂停震爆弹的⼏天后，⼜授权警⽅在 " 城市暴⼒ " 中使⽤它。

### Page 4

⼈们聚集在⾥昂旅游办公室前，该办公室于 2026 年 10 ⽉ 17 ⽇⽰威中被纵⽕。图源：法新社
爱丽舍宫盯着的，是债券市场那块屏幕
街头的⽕，⻢克⻰⻅过。 2018 年⻅过， 2023 年⻅过。这⼀次真正让他和总理勒科尔尼睡不着
的，是另⼀块屏幕。
9 ⽉ 18 ⽇，欧洲评级机构 Scope 把法国主权信⽤评级从 AA- 下调到 A+ ，与惠誉、标普站到了同⼀
档。同⼀天，法国 10 年期国债收益率⼀度冲到 4.57% ， 2008 年以来最⾼；法国国债相对德国国
债的利差达到 105 个基点， 14 年来第⼀次越过 100 这道关⼝。
10 ⽉ 1 ⽇， 2027 年预算案公布当天， 10 年期收益率摸到 4.96% ， 2002 年 7 ⽉以来最⾼，离 5% 只
差⼀步。到 10 ⽉初，法德利差⼀度扩⼤到 133 个基点， 2012 年欧债危机以来最阔。
⼀个曾经和德国⼀起构成欧元区 " ⽆⻛险核⼼ " 的国家，现在的借钱成本已经⾼于希腊、意⼤利、
西班⽛和葡萄⽛。
法国国家统计与经济研究所的数据显⽰，截⾄今年⼆季度末，法国公共债务 3.596 万亿欧元，占

GDP 的 119% 。财政部⾃⼰的预测更差： 2026 年 119.3% ， 2027 年 121.7% ，是欧盟 60% 上限的两
倍。⾚字率 2025 年为 5.1% ， 2026 年预计升到 5.4% ，⽐原定的 5.0% ⽬标⾼出⼀截，那个⽬标实
际上已经被放弃；经济增⻓预期今年已被下调三次，从 0.7% 降到 0.5% 。
更⿇烦的是利息。财政部⻓莱斯库尔在公布预算时说，明年仅付息就要吃掉⾚字的⼀半以上，
到本⼗年末会达到 1000 亿欧元。他说完这句，⼜补了⼀句 " 法国的签名是可靠的 " 。同⼀天，他在
考虑另⼀件事：少发⻓债，多发短债。
投标倍数的变化⽐官员的表态更诚实。法国⻓期国债的投标倍数从 2025 年的 3.2 掉到 2.5 ，短期
国债从 3.2 掉到 2.1 ；发⾏的平均期限在 6 ⽉已缩到 5 年，⽐ 2024 年初短了半年。买家在变短，也
在变挑。
法国的问题不是债务太⾼，⽽是没有⼈能决定怎么还。
希腊债务⽐法国⾼，意⼤利也⽐法国⾼。可希腊已经削过⼀轮，意⼤利⾄少有⼀个稳定的政

府。法国是四件事同时发⽣：⾼债务、低增⻓、碎⽚化的议会、半年后的⼤选。
五⼗三年没平衡过预算，法国⽋钱不是从今天开始的

### Page 5

把时间拉⻓，会看到另⼀幅图景。
法国上⼀次实现预算平衡是 1973 年，上⼀次达到欧盟 3% ⾚字上限是 2019 年。最近三年，⾚字率
⼀直在 5% 以上。
2017 年⻢克⻰刚上台时，法国公共债务还不到 GDP 的 100% ，⾚字率 2.8% 。当时他被视为那个
能把法国 " 改好 " 的⼈。
2018 年冬天， " ⻩背⼼ " 烧了⾹榭丽舍，政府撤回了燃油税。 2020 年疫情，法国推出 " ⽆论代价如
何 " 的全⾯兜底。 2023 年退休年龄从 62 岁推到 64 岁，全国抗议持续数⽉。 2024 年欧洲议会选举
后解散国⺠议会，换来⼀个没有任何⼀党过半的议会。当年 12 ⽉，巴尼耶政府因预算被推翻；
2025 年，⻉鲁政府重演了同⼀幕。勒科尔尼是第三个。
每⼀次危机，法国都⽤借债买了⼀次平静。平静越来越短，账单越来越⻓。
现在法国的公共⽀出占 GDP 的 57% 。福利、公务员⼯资、医保、养⽼⾦，每⼀项都是刚性的。
想削减，街上就着⽕；不削减，债市就着⽕。世界报⽤ " 经济陷阱正在收紧 " 形容这个局⾯：⼀边

要增加投资推动再⼯业化，⼀边要削减不断侵蚀财政空间的⾚字，两边都要钱。
中东的战争，正好打在法国最软的地⽅
债务是过去五⼗三年⼀层层攒下来的。今年的⿇烦，是在这个存量上⼜新添了⼀层。
今年 2 ⽉ 28 ⽇，中东战事爆发。霍尔⽊兹海峡被卡住，这条通道承载全球约 20% 的碳氢化合物消
费。
法兰西银⾏ 6 ⽉的《⾦融稳定报告》给了⼀个很克制的判断：这⼀次和 2022 年不⼀样。油⽓库存
⽐当年⾼得多，东亚需求⼤幅收缩，液化天然⽓的冲击远⼩于俄乌战争之后，所以原油价格涨
得 " 相对温和 " 。
成品油不讲这个道理。欧洲柴油价格较年初翻了⼀倍，法国柴油零售价创下每升 2.41 欧元的纪
录，德国是 2.471 欧元。今年 3 ⽉到 8 ⽉，中东柴油出⼝从每天 80 万桶掉到 40 万桶，⽽ 2025 年中
东满⾜了欧洲四成以上的柴油进⼝。
9 ⽉，欧元区通胀率升到 3.8% ， 8 ⽉还是 3.2% ，能源价格同⽐涨 18.8% 。法国的 CPI 从 8 ⽉的
2.4% 跳到 9 ⽉的 3.1% 。

对普通家庭来说，前⼏年政府发的那些补贴，基本被物价吃⼲净了。
法国超过 60% 的进⼝柴油要经过霍尔⽊兹海峡和苏伊⼠运河，约 15% 的原油来⾃中东。⼀条航
道⾛不通，加油站⻔⼝就开始排队。
最要命的⼀环藏在财政合同⾥。法国⼤约⼗分之⼀的债务是指数化债券，通胀⼀起来，这部分
债务的本⾦⾃动变⼤；养⽼⾦和家庭补贴也按通胀上调。收⼊端呢，燃油税按销量收，油价⼀
⾼，销量下来，税收反⽽少了。
通胀每多拖⼀个季度，法国的债务就⾃动多背⼀层。
法兰西银⾏算过账：按 4 ⽉的数据，这场战争会让 2026 年的财政平衡多出 60 亿欧元缺⼝，其中
44 亿来⾃宏观环境恶化，仅利息⽀出就要多掏 36 亿。这还是基准情景。如果战事拖⻓，进⼊ " 严

### Page 6

重情景 " ，法国 2026 年增⻓为零， 2027 年萎缩 0.1% ，通胀在 2026 年冲到 4% 。
所以 " 中东通胀 " 同时打到法国的三个地⽅：家庭的账单、政府的⽀出、央⾏的利率。
⻢克⻰⻜往海湾的那天，预算案还在议会等他
10 ⽉ 18 ⽇晚，⻢克⻰发表电视讲话，谴责暴⼒，说 " 任何理由都不能为这些⾏为开脱 " 。他也承
认，抗议反映了⺠众对⽣活成本和油价的担忧，并要求警⽅对学⽣保持 " 相称 " 。
第⼆天，他⻜往科威特、阿联酋、巴林、卡塔尔，去谈⻓期的油⽓供应协议。
这个⾏程安排本⾝就是⼀幅画：他能管住外⾯的油轮，管不住⾥⾯的选票。
摆在议会桌上的是 540 亿欧元的财政整顿⽅案，其中 430 亿是 2027 年新增的。内容包括冻结公务
员⼯资的指数化（⼯会说这已是连续第四年）、冻结除最低档以外的养⽼⾦、压缩地⽅政府⽀出
和医保开⽀、下调企业社保缴费的税收优惠，还有把退休⼈员的税收减免上限从 4439 欧元降到
3000 欧元。国防是唯⼀的例外，明年还要再加 64 亿。

勒科尔尼的政府在国⺠议会没有多数。⽅案要在 12 ⽉前过，只有两条路：拿到⾜够多的妥协
票，或者动⽤宪法第 49.3 条强⾏通过，然后承受不信任投票。
荷兰的 ABN AMRO 给出的基准判断是： 540 亿⾥最后能活下来 350 到 400 亿，实际落地 250 到 300
亿， 2027 年的⾚字率还是 5.4% 左右。
过去两年，已经有两任总理因为预算被赶下台。勒科尔尼是第三个。
他在电视讲话⾥说了⼀句很实在的话： " 这是国家疲惫的时刻。我们正处在⼀场永久危机的氛围
⾥：战争、通胀、购买⼒。 "
明年 4 ⽉ 18 ⽇，才是这场危机的定价⽇
法国总统选举⾸轮定在 2027 年 4 ⽉ 18 ⽇，第⼆轮 5 ⽉ 2 ⽇。⻢克⻰不能再选。
9 ⽉的 Ipsos ⺠调显⽰，他的⽀持率是 16% ， 81% 的受访者对他的施政表现持负⾯评价。同⼀时
期的多份⺠调⾥，勒庞在⾸轮拿到 33% 到 36% ，梅朗雄和菲利普在争第⼆名。
债券市场现在要价的，是⼀个概率：明年之后，法国还有没有⼈敢削⾚字。⾄于候选⼈的政策

细节，反倒没多少⼈细看。
勒庞的⽅案是把公共债务上限写进法律，定在 GDP 的 60% ，同时降低部分⼯种的退休年龄、下
调能源税。梅朗雄的⽅案是让欧洲央⾏免掉⼀部分法国国债的利息。在多数市场⼈⼠看来，前
者会把法国直接推⼊衰退，后者在欧元区的法律框架⾥⾛不通。
市场其实不太在意谁当选。它在意的是，⽆论谁当选，紧缩都会被打折。凯投宏观的⾸席欧洲
经济学家肯宁厄姆在报告⾥写得很直⽩：投资者还担⼼选举后出现更强的财政⺠粹主义， " 利差
在选举前或选举后⼤幅⾛阔的⻛险都很⼤ " 。
这不是法国⼀家的事。 10 ⽉初，欧元跌到 1.12 美元， 2025 年 5 ⽉以来最弱。欧洲央⾏ 6 ⽉ 11 ⽇已
经加息 25 个基点，市场还在押年底前再加⼀到两次。意⼤利的利差反⽽⽐法国窄，罗⻢在这场
⻛暴⾥成了旁观者。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

间点。
2027 年 4 ⽉ 18 ⽇，总统选举⾸轮。
利摩⽇那座 43 ⽶的钟楼塌下来的时候，消防员还在往⽕⾥喷⽔。法国要修的不只是那座钟楼。
街头的⽕会灭。债市那块屏幕上的数字，不会。
限免

## Document 188: 9月美国CPI数据点评：通胀超预期之下，加息预期剧烈波动

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月美国CPI数据点评：通胀超预期之下，加息预期剧烈波动.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

此报告最后部分的分析师披露、商业关系披露和免责声明为报告的一部分，必须阅读。
下载本公司之研究报告，可从彭博搜寻NH BCM  或 登录研究部网站https://research.bocomgroup.com 
交银国际研究 
宏观策略

2026年10月16日
全球宏观 
通胀超预期之下，加息预期剧烈波动——9月美国CPI数据点评
美国通胀回升，核心通胀超预期，核心价格压力有所扩散。9月美国CPI环比
上涨0.6%，较8月的0.4%进一步加速，同比由3.4%升至3.7%，高于市场预期
的3.6%；核心CPI环比上涨0.3%，与8月持平，高于预期的0.2%，同比由8
月的2.4%升至2.6%。总体通胀回升主要由能源推动，但核心通胀环比上涨显
示，通胀回落进程面临新的阻力。
食品通胀整体温和，能源继续成为推升总通胀的主要力量。 食品价格环比持
平、同比上涨2.5%，其中家庭食品下降0.1%，外出就餐上涨0.2%。能源价格
环比上涨5.2%、同比上涨20.4%，其中汽油环比大涨9.1%、同比上涨34.2%，
贡献当月CPI涨幅的一半以上；燃油价格环比上涨15.3%。中东地缘冲突导致
能源供应持续受扰，汽柴油价格高企不仅直接推升通胀，还可能通过运输及
生产成本向其他商品价格传导。
核心商品通胀有所加速，核心服务通胀粘性增强。核心商品环比上涨0.3%，
高于8月的0.1%，同比上涨0.7%。其中二手车价格上涨0.7%，新车价格上涨
0.2%，服装上涨0.3%，医疗护理商品则下降0.7%。核心服务环比由0.3%升至
0.4%、同比上涨3.1%。其中住房环比上涨0.4%，业主等价租金上涨0.3%，住
宿上涨2.0%；交通服务上涨0.6%，航空票价上涨1.2%、同比涨22.4%；汽车
维修、医院服务分别上涨0.7%，教育上涨0.6%。住房仍是核心通胀的重要支
撑，而交通、教育及部分医疗服务价格上涨，显示剔除住房后的服务通胀压
力也不容忽视。
展望未来，能源冲击与服务价格粘性或使通胀在年底前维持高位。10月汽柴
油价格仍是最大不确定性，其向运输、食品和商品价格的传导可能尚未结束。
到年底，若油价维持高位，总体CPI同比或持续高于3%，核心通胀回落也将
较为缓慢。进入2027年，若中东能源供应改善、住房租金滞后回落及工资增
速放缓，通胀有望重新进入下降通道；反之，能源与关税冲击的持续传导可
能使通胀高位运行更长时间。
美联储政策方面，通胀超预期推动美联储进一步加息的概率剧烈波动。 数据
公布前，市场对10月加息25基点的定价约为两成，公布后一度升至45%—
50%，随后回落到约两成多的水平。我们认为，考虑到能源供给冲击无法通
过加息直接解决，叠加就业增长有所放缓，美联储仍可能选择10月暂时观
望，但通胀二轮传导风险有所上升，12月加息仍是基准情形。
市场反应：呈现股市下跌、美元走强的特征。 CPI公布当日收盘，道指、标普
500和纳指全线下跌，分别下跌约0.09%、1.11%和1.88%，科技股特别是半导
体承压明显，金融股受银行财报影响表现分化，能源相关资产则受到高油价
支撑。2年期美债收益率冲高回落到收盘上行约3基点，10年期美债收益率盘
中上升，但收盘下降2个基点；美元指数上涨约0.04%；黄金下跌约1.6%；布
伦特原油维持在每桶106美元附近。整体来看，市场重新定价美联储加息路
径，利率敏感型资产面临压力。


王博群, PhD, FRM
hugh.wang@bocomgroup.com
(852) 3766 1853

评级定义
分析员个股评级定义 : 分析员行业评级定义：
买入：预期个股未来12个月的总回报高于相关行业。
中性：预期个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业一致。
沽出：预期个股未来12个月的总回报低于相关行业
无评级：对于个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业的比较，
分析员并无确信观点。
领先：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数具吸引力。
同步：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现与大盘标杆
指数一致。
落后：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数不具吸引力。
香港市场的标杆指数为恒生综合指数，A股市场的标杆指数
为MSCI中国A股指数，美国上市中概股的标杆指数为标普美
国中概股50（美元）指数

## Document 250: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Acter, an established 
multinational cleanroom engineering, procurement, construction and 
management (EPCM) turnkey service provider, with a Buy (1) rating. Its 
core business includes cleanroom electromechanical engineering, 
water/gas/chemical delivery systems, and other cleanroom services. We 
believe now is a good time to revisit the stock as it underperformed the 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly on weaker-than-expected 2Q26 
results and soft sector sentiment. Given its stronger revenue momentum 
over recent months and upcoming TSMC results on 15 October, which may 
drive sentiment, we suggest investors to buy the stock. We forecast a 
24.6% revenue CAGR and 36.9% net profit CAGR over 2026-28E.

Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, 
and rapid cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order 
backlog. Its backlog reached TWD66bn in July 2026 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 
and TWD30bn in 2022 (15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the 
global cleanroom technology market’s forecast CAGR over 2023-2032E, 
according to Precedence Research.

Strong competitive edge supports order share gains across multiple 
segments. Acter’s competitive edge lies in 1) intelligent management 
enabled by digital twins and AI, using 3D building information modelling 
(BIM) and AI-driven digital twin management; 2) intellectualisation of green 
engineering, which comprises green design, green procurement, and green 
techniques; 3) experience across multiple industries (ie, data centres and 
electromechanical engineering for the consumer industry) which supports 
better integration; and 4) a complete network, enabling on-the-spot support. 
Acter believes it has the highest market share among the operators of three 
major US data centre players due largely to its capabilities in liquid cooling 
and its expertise in electromechanical and cleanroom engineering.

Catalysts: Near-term: project wins from key clients, supporting stronger 
monthly revenue momentum; mid- to long-term: an improving operating-
margin trend and a resilient order backlog.

Valuation: We are the first non-domestic broker to cover Acter. We initiate 
coverage with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target PER of 20x 
(equivalent to a 0.54x PEG over 2026-28E). We believe our valuation is 
fair, given Acter’s strong earnings visibility, which we believe warrants a 
rerating. Our TP is supported by a DCF valuation of TWD1,454.

Risks: 1) weaker-than-expected expansion in the semiconductor industry; 
and 2) project delays or cancellations.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

Acter: net profit and net-profit growth    
We forecast Acter to record a 36.9% net profit CAGR over 
2026-28E, on the back of a 24.6% revenue CAGR over the 
same period, driven by 1) larger project scale, as strong 
demand for cleanroom EPCM services from the high-tech 
sector is likely to support continued growth in Acter’s order 
backlog and 2) improving gross margins and operating 
efficiency. We expect operating margin to expand by 2.0pp 
in 2027E and 1.3pp in 2028E, driven by favourable gross 
margins on new projects and operating leverage.

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Valuation   Acter: 1-year-forward PER bands    
Our 12-month TP of TWD1,415 is based on a target PER 
of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Our 
target multiple of 20x is higher than the stock’s past-3-year 
1-year-forward PER (12.2x) and past-5-year 1-year-
forward PER (9.6x) and implies a PEG of 0.54x on our 
36.9% net profit CAGR forecast over 2026-28E. We 
believe the stock deserves a rerating in the next 12 
months, largely supported by resilient cleanroom demand 
globally.

Our TP is also supported by an alternative DCF-derived 
valuation of TWD1,454/share.

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

Earnings revisions

Acter: Bloomberg consensus EPS forecast revisions   
Bloomberg consensus EPS estimates for 2026E and 
2027E have risen 24.8% and 24.3% YTD, respectively. 
This is likely due to upward revisions to order backlog and 
earnings forecasts following better-than-expected industry 
outlook. We are the first foreign broker to cover Acter.

Company profile 
Established in 1979, Acter Group (Acter) is a leading engineering services provider specialising in 
cleanroom, electromechanical, and high-tech facility integration solutions for semiconductor, 
optoelectronics, biotech, green energy, and advanced manufacturing clients across Taiwan, China and 
the rest of the world. As of 2025, its revenue comprised cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas 
and chemical delivery systems (20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and 
high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

### Page 5

5

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acting on the demand for cleanrooms 
Why are cleanrooms a bottleneck in the high-tech 
industry? 
In recent years, cleanrooms have become a critical bottleneck for high-tech industries, 
especially semiconductor manufacturing, OSAT, AI hardware, and PCB, mainly because of 
its indispensable nature, severe supply-demand imbalance, labour shortages, and high 
technical and regulatory barriers.

Cleanrooms are an indispensable core infrastructure as they determine product 
yield and quality stability. Advanced manufacturing nodes in semiconductors, 
optoelectronics, and biopharmaceuticals require highly particle-free, constant-temperature, 
and constant-humidity environments to ensure reliable production. As process 
technologies continue to advance, such as 2nm node (or even newer nodes) and 
advanced packaging, existing cleanrooms must be rebuilt, and the complexity of building 
new ones thus increases. This keeps overall demand for cleanrooms at high levels.

A severe global supply-demand imbalance with demand for cleanroom construction 
outpacing supply. The surge in AI servers, high-performance computing (HPC), and high-
bandwidth memory (HBM) has triggered a global expansion for front-end wafer fabs, 
packaging and testing facilities, and PCB manufacturing. Order visibility remains extremely 
strong across the industry. According to Markets and Markets, the global cleanroom market 
size is set to expand at a CAGR of 6.6% over 2025-31E. At the same time, the global 
semiconductor cleanroom market size is set to grow at a CAGR of 8.0% over 2025-30E. 
For instance, Acter’s order backlog exceeded TWD66bn at the end of July 2026, up from 
TWD46bn at the end of 2025, showing the urgency of demand for cleanroom construction.

Global: cleanroom market size and growth      Global: semiconductor cleanroom market size and growth

Source: Markets and Markets  Source: Markets and Markets

Human resources are an additional limitation to capacity growth. Cleanroom 
engineering is highly technical and labour-intensive, relying on specialised on-site 
installation and supervision that cannot be replaced by robots. At the same time, the 
industry faces severe labour shortages and an aging workforce, and younger generations 
are reluctant to join field operations. Cross-border staff deployment is also less cost-
efficient than local hiring.

Cleanrooms involve complex system integration, creating high barriers to entry. A 
cleanroom construction includes 4 sequential phases, where each phase usually requires 
totally different areas of expertise. Therefore, the competitive landscape in each area 
differs. The process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the physical 
plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for essential utility 
generation, where Acter is one of the leading global experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
network and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. As

Cleanrooms are a 
bottleneck in the high-

### Page 6

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
global supply chains restructure under N+1 (domestic market + 1) strategies, companies 
building overseas facilities in places like the US face strict and complicated licensing 
processes, particularly on fire safety and hazardous chemicals. Without experience dealing 
with such engineering and safety issues, construction timelines can be severely delayed.

As a result, even with advanced equipment and chip designs, a high-tech company cannot 
begin production without adequate cleanroom space, skilled engineers, and system-
integration capabilities. Therefore, cleanroom engineering contractors hold significant 
bargaining power and capacity allocation leverage in today's tech supply chain.

IC foundry 
In the foundry sector, where the cleanroom supply-demand shortage is the tightest, project 
activity remains centred on advanced process capacity expansion in Taiwan and Arizona. 
Simultaneously, domestic advanced node development remains intense, with multi-phase 
fab builds advancing across Hsinchu, Taichung, and Kaohsiung. Local cleanroom and MEP 
integration demand is further boosted by advanced packaging facilities in Taiwan (such as 
Chiayi and Tainan) and upcoming projects in the US are set to contribute revenue for the 
cleanroom providers in the following years.

Memory 
The memory segment is exhibiting robust momentum as well, mainly catalysed by HBM 
capacity additions. Projects such as the plant in Japan, transitioned to contribution for the 
cleanroom EPCM providers starting from 2026. In Taiwan, turnkey engineering contracts in 
Miaoli are also actively underway. This trend is reinforced by stronger-than-expected 
expansion activity from memory producers in China.

OSAT,  PCB and ODM/OEM 
For OSAT companies, demand is also surging due to the spill-over effect of advanced 
packaging protocols and N+1 supply chain restructuring. The OSAT names are expanding 
their footprint both in Southern Taiwan and in overseas locations including in the US and 
Southeast Asia. Major engineering contracts signed between OSAT clients and Acter show 
stable long-term workload for strong cleanroom demand over our forecast horizon. 
Similarly, overseas expansion for PCB manufacturing is peaking in Southeast Asia, 
especially in Thailand. We expect Chinese PCB players to also increase their capacity, just 
like memory companies in the past year. Except for front-end and back-end chip 
manufacturing, ODM/OEM and data centre infrastructure is also a key growth engine for 
the EPCM industry. Major Taiwanese ODMs are building production facilities in Texas and 
Mexico to meet local manufacturing needs. Cleanroom contractors, such as Acter, 
providing MEP and system integration in Texas are directly benefiting from these projects. 
The shift toward liquid cooling systems has also increased engineering complexity, 
favouring few cleanroom companies with the expertise to deliver complex integration for 
CSPs in both North America and Southeast Asia.

Acter: an integrated cleanroom solutions provider  
Acter Group (Acter), an integrated cleanroom solutions provider, maintains its competitive 
positioning within the high-tech cleanroom and MEP engineering sector, driven by its group 
synergy and global footprint. Acter operates as a comprehensive vendor through a group 
business model. The parent company, Acter, supervises overall cleanroom and MEP 
services, while subsidiary Nova Technology (Novatech, 6613 TT, not rated) mainly provides 
chemical primary system and hook-up, and Novatech’s subsidiary Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated) provides gas primary system, hook-up and equipment. In China, Acter operates 
through Acter Technology Integration Group (603163 CH, not rated) and Suzhou Winmax 
(not listed) for Chinese EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management) 
and equipment business. Except for C&S (civil and structural) works, where the margin is 
low (7-8%) and the contract duration is long, Acter’s services span nearly the entire 
cleanroom construction process. Its one-stop solutions model supports customer retention 
Acter provides

solutions via various 
subsidiaries

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
and improves bid-win rates, particularly for overseas expansion, where customers have 
few reliable providers to choose from.

Acter: organisational chart

Source: Company, Daiwa

To avoid the risk of over-concentration, Acter diversifies its client portfolio across 
semiconductor foundries, memory, PCB, OSAT, ODM hardware assembly, CSP data 
centres and the consumer industry. By sector, semiconductor projects represent the largest 
share of Acter’s portfolio, accounting for 66% of total revenue in 1H26, vs. 69% in 2025. 
Foundry manufacturing is the largest source of demand for cleanrooms in Taiwan, 
especially as advanced-node capacity expands at sites in Hsinchu, Taichung and 
Kaohsiung. In the memory segment, the company executes turnkey engineering builds to 
expand its capacity aggressively, creating cleanroom opportunities across Taiwan, Japan 
and Singapore. This multi-sector coverage decreases the risk of cyclical downturns in any 
individual segment, in our view.

By establishing 33 operating entities across 11 international regions, the company 
effectively captures capex migration driven by global N+1 supply chain diversification. This 
allows Acter to support Taiwanese and international clients as they expand manufacturing 
capacity from Taiwan into Southeast Asia, Japan and the US. Due to the complexity of 
advanced-node IC manufacturing, packaging and testing, it is especially important for 
these Taiwanese semiconductor companies to work with familiar cleanroom builders when 
expanding in the US. This is one of the reasons why Taiwanese cleanroom companies are 
receiving overseas orders and starting to set up global bases, in our view. Despite higher 
management, material, and manpower costs in the US, most cleanroom companies are 
benefitting from the trend by improving margins, given the higher complexity and risks to 
operate overseas. Across overseas hubs in Texas and Phoenix (expected to begin from 
2027), Acter prioritises local hiring and partners with regional civil contractors to shorten 
the learning curve and to avoid the high overhead. To further address the potential labour 
shortages in the future, the company has established cross-border academic and 
vocational partnerships to bring Southeast Asian talent to Taiwan for technical training 
before deploying them back to their home markets, securing future human capacity to 
support global expansion projects.

By product, cleanroom electromechanical services remain Acter’s largest source of 
revenue (accounting for 66% of 1H26 revenue, per the company). These services include 
cleanroom architectural and MEP engineering, which powers airflow, filtration, 
environmental regulation and utility controls. Water, gas and chemical delivery systems are 
its second-largest source of revenue (accounting for 24% of 1H26 revenue, per the 
company). Contributed mainly by Novatech (6613 TT, not rated) and Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated), these services cover the main systems and hook-up systems, for  ultra-pure water 
(UPW), specialty gases, chemical piping, etc., all of which require highly precise build-up 
work. Except for cleanroom electromechanical and water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems, the rest of Acter’s revenue comes from the consumer industry services 
electromechanical (5%), biomedical (3%), and high-tech equipment and materials sales 
and services (1%).

Acter: revenue by product

Source: Company, Daiwa

Technically, Acter integrates digital transformation and green engineering into its business 
model, helping it differentiate from peers. Acter deploys digital twin technology and 1:1 3D 
BIM modelling to detect piping interferences and optimise spatial layout before installation, 
minimising schedule delays. This makes Acter a more reliable option, in our view, and thus 
being a favourable supplier, especially for the semiconductor clients that have extremely 
high quality and swiftness criterion.

Bright outlook: strong order visibility 
Rising orders on hand due to supply-demand imbalance for cleanrooms

TWD66bn in July 2026. We expect this figure to further increase in the following quarters, 
given the supply-demand imbalance in the cleanroom sector. This is composed of 
semiconductor (63%), electronic assembly (11%), consumer (9%) and others (17%), per 
the company. In 2H25, the company accelerated the digestion of small-scale projects 
below TWD1bn, and, as a result, starting from 1H26, it successfully shifted its project

Back-end packaging, cloud infrastructure and hardware assembly are additional growth

qualified supplier status with US CSPs, providing mechanical, electrical and plumbing 
(MEP) integration, especially for those data centres that implement liquid-cooling systems. 
The company also wins factory construction projects for Taiwanese ODMs across the US 
and Southeast Asia, as well as PCB facility projects in Thailand and China. Non-tech 
sectors, including biotechnology, commercial buildings, smart logistics hubs and medical 
facilities, comprise 9% of the total backlog, per management.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Competitive advantages 
High entry barriers to the segment 
Comprehensive product portfolio, empowered by subsidiaries 
Acter provides a one-stop EPCM solution, leveraging its subsidiaries to deliver complete 
solutions. Within the group, the parent company Acter focuses on overall cleanroom and 
MEP system integration; subsidiary Novatech provides primary chemical systems and 
hook-up systems; and sub-subsidiary Rayzer specialises in primary specialty-gas systems 
and hook-up systems. In China, Suzhou Winmax, also a subsidiary of Novatech, focuses 
on the supply of wet processing and chemical handling equipment. This integrated model 
covers cleanroom engineering integration, water, power, chemical and gas-utility systems, 
as well as post-completion validation and maintenance, ensuring cross-system 
compatibility while mitigating risks associated with multi-contractor hiring.

Under global supply chain diversification trends, this unified group strategy strengthens 
order-winning capabilities in overseas markets. Clients expanding internationally face 
regulatory, linguistic and supply-chain complexities, favouring partners that offer one-stop 
services with a proven cross-border track record.

A diverse client base helps Acter weather economic cycles 
Acter’s main competitive advantage is its diversification of clients by industry and region. 
This guides the company through the peaks and troughs of the economy, which is 
especially crucial given the strong cyclicality of the semiconductor/high-tech industry.  Acter 
serves a broad client base across semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, electronics assembly 
(ODM), CSP data centres, biopharmaceuticals and even the consumer industry (including 
commercial real estate and logistics), whereas many peers rely on a few large foundry 
clients. When semiconductor demand slows, capacity expansion in the PCB or data centre 
sectors offset the decline. Additionally, Acter avoids concentration risk and margin 
compression from major foundry accounts, as projects from leading foundries yield lower 
margins; by serving niche markets like US-based CSPs, the company achieves higher 
gross margins.

Geographically, Acter operates across Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia, the US and other 
countries. The company has OSAT and foundry customers in Taiwan and China, PCB 
clients expanding into Thailand, and ODM and data centre clients establishing operations 
in the US. Across different countries, local hiring and local supply chain implementation 
can further reduce costs and increase efficiency relative to peers reliant on a cross-border 
labour force.

Labour shortages 
Amid global labour shortages, Acter minimises the operational impact and turns workforce 
constraints into a moat through localised talent development. Since engineering labour 
cannot easily be automated, Acter has built a cross-border talent pipeline via industry-
academia partnerships. The company recruits Southeast Asian students for specialised 
M&E engineering training in Taiwan, deploying them to global project sites after their 
graduation to solve labour bottlenecks. This structure maintains an engineering team with 
an average tenure of more than 7 years, creating an operational barrier in an industry with 
a high employee turnover rate.

Compared with peers relying on high-cost personnel sent from Taiwan, we note that Acter’s 
local hiring strategy creates cost and efficiency advantages. Prioritising compliant local 
hiring across overseas markets reduces travel expenses while accelerating integration with 
local supply chains and regulations. Localised teams also handle post-completion services 
and technical support directly on-site, building long-term client trust in overseas markets.

services is limited due to

### Page 11

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Numerous competitors, each with different specialisation 
Acter faces different competitors across its main product lines. In the EPCM segment, 
including cleanroom and MEP works, key competitors include UIS (2404 TT, not rated), 
L&K (6139 TT, not rated), Yankey (6691 TT, not rated) and Jiuhan (6903 TT, not rated). 
Despite having business models similar to Acter, UIS, Yankey and Jiuhan focus more on 
major semiconductor players in both Taiwan and the US, and L&K concentrates on ASEAN 
semiconductor projects. In the equipment/material sales and agency space, Acter mainly 
competes with Marketech (6196 TT, not rated). In the water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems area, through Novatech and Rayzher, Acter group competes with Marketech, 
Mega Union (6944 TT, not rated), Taiwan Puritic (6826 TT, not rated), Trusval (6667 TT, not 
rated) and Cica-Huntek (6725 TT, not rated).

In our view, despite the competitive environment, Acter maintains a formidable competitive 
moat built on several key advantages. We attribute this to its more than 45 year experience 
in the industry, enabling it to develop specialised expertise that is hard for competitors to 
replicate.

Financial analysis 
We forecast the company’s net profit to rise at a CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E, driven by 
a revenue CAGR of 24.6% due to surging demand from the high-tech industry (an increase 
in the order backlog to TWD66bn at the end of July 2026 vs. TWD46bn at end-2025) and a 
3.3pp expansion in operating margin over 2026-28E, driven by the gross margin 
improvement from more favourable project wins and operating leverage.

Strong order visibility, with revenue expected to grow 
across all sectors over 2026-28E 
We forecast Acter’s revenue to increase at a CAGR of 24.6% over 2026-28E, driven by 
scale expansion and a growing demand from various industries, including semiconductor, 
electronic assembly, optoelectronics and others.

Acter: revenue growth trend

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Gross- and operating-margin expansion trend  
Over 2026-28E, we forecast Acter’s gross and operating margins to expand on rising 
revenue scale and a higher complexity of cleanrooms for advanced manufacturing. We 
note that the gross margin from high-tech cleanrooms is higher than that from consumer 
industries, such as commercial buildings and biopharmaceuticals. Thus, we estimate the 
company’s gross margin to reach 26.0% in 2028E from 18.9% in 2025 and its operating 
margin to reach 21.6% in 2028E from 13.7% in 2025.

Intact demand to support revenue growth over our 
forecast horizon; ROE expansion underway 
We forecast Acter’s total cash and equivalents to reach TWD16,838m (36.8% of its total 
assets) in 2026E and increase to TWD27,839m in 2028E (42.8% of total assets) with net 
cash likely to come in at TWD16,244m in 2026E and TWD27,245m in 2028E.

After factoring in our 2027 and 2028 capex assumptions of TWD180m, we expect Acter’s 
free cash flow (FCF) to improve to TWD12,129m in 2028E, from TWD5,763m in 2026E. 
We believe the capacity expansion cost will initially be low and that the company will 
continue to benefit from the better profitability.

We expect Acter’s ROE to improve to a record-high of 56.8% in 2028E from 29.3% in 2025 
(following steady annual increases to 46.8% in 2026E and 54.9% in 2027E), which we 
attribute to margin expansion from the revenue size increase and gross margin expansion 
led by increases in cleanroom complexity.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Valuation and risks  
Undemanding valuation, alongside its leading global 
positions in the OSAT and memory segments and an 
optimistic industry outlook  
We initiate coverage of Acter with a Buy (1) rating and 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based 
on a target PER of 20x applied to our one-year-forward EPS forecast. This target multiple 
is higher than the average of its past-3-year 1-year-forward PER of 12.2x (ranged from 
7.4x to 23.5x) and the average of its past-5-year 1-year-forward PER of 9.6x (ranged from 
4.4x to 23.5x), which we view as undemanding as we forecast a 36.9% net profit CAGR 
over 2026-28E, implying a 0.54x PEG. This is supported by its business expansion in the 
next 2-3 years. We see potential for a rerating of the stock, given Acter’s global leadership 
in cleanroom EPCM, particularly in the OSAT and memory industries.

The stock is trading currently at a 15.8x 1-year-forward PER vs. its earnings CAGR of 
36.9% over 2026-28E, translating to a 0.43x PEG ratio. Its share price has underperformed 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly due to its weaker-than-expected 2Q26 results. 
However, given the potential improvement in sector sentiment following TSMC’s results on 
15 October 2026, along with the likelihood that Acter’s revenue momentum will strengthen 
in the coming months, we recommend that investors buy the stock.

Acter: past-3-year-forward PER (average: 12.0x)    Acter: past-5-year-forward PER (average: 9.5x)

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates  Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates

We compare Acter with other leading cleanroom EPCM companies such as UIS (2404 TT, 
TWD1,330.0, not rated), L&K (6139 TT, TWD787.0, not rated) and Yankey (6691 TT, 
TWD776.0, not rated). UIS, L&K and Yankey are trading at a PER of 14.7x (earnings 
CAGR of 40.7% over 2025-27E), 9.5x (earnings CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E) and 
15.1x (earnings CAGR of 53.6% over 2025-27E), on their 1-year-forward EPS, 
respectively.

Acter’s peers are trading at 18.7x for 2026E and 14.2x for 2027E. We forecast Acter to 
deliver an EPS CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E vs. 31.5% CAGR for the segment over the 
same period.

We believe our target one-year-forward PER of 20x (equivalent to a 0.54x PEG using 
2026-28E EPS CAGR) is undemanding as: 1) Acter is ramping up capacity to meet 
growing demand from high-tech customers and is in an expansion cycle; and 2) its 
leading position among OSAT and memory clients warrants a valuation premium, 
compared to its global peers.

Risks to our call  
Primary/key risks: 
Weaker-than-expected expansion in semiconductor industry 
The biggest downside risk to our call for Acter is weaker-than-expected expansion from 
semiconductor clients, especially in advanced-node foundries, advanced packaging and 
OSAT. If global demand for AI servers, HPC or HBM slows, or if clients delay fab build-ups 
and overseas expansion plans, it will directly pressure order inflows and backlog 
conversion for Acter.

Project execution delays or cancellations 
Acter’s revenue recognition is tied to large, complex EPCM and integrated engineering 
projects, which are often subject to customer-driven delays. Any postponement in project 
Our 12-month TP of 
TWD1,415 is also 
supported by our DCF 
valuation

### Page 18

18

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
schedule, construction bottlenecks or cancellation of large projects could defer revenue 
recognition and hurt overall profit. This risk is more obvious in overseas projects, where 
licensing, fire-safety approvals and hazardous-chemical regulations are more complicated.

Higher-than-expected raw-material price hikes 
Raw-material costs (especially copper and oil-related costs) account for 50-60% of COGS 
and thus the price volatility makes costs difficult to manage. Acter needs to maintain 
supplier relations, negotiate appropriate price-adjustment mechanisms with clients, and 
invest in R&D to identify composite material substitutes, thereby reducing reliance on any 
single component.

Secondary risks:  
Labour issues or larger-than-expected increases in labour costs  
Cleanroom engineering is highly labour-intensive and relies on on-site installation and 
supervision. Although Acter has built a local hiring and talent-development model, a larger-
than-expected shortage of engineers or rising wage pressure could still raise costs and 
constrain capacity expansion. Any inability to secure enough skilled labour may also limit 
the company’s ability to convert orders into revenue in time.

Margin pressure from lower-margin projects or mix shift 
Acter’s business mix includes a few low-margin C&S services as well as larger EPCM and 
system integration contracts. If the company takes on a higher proportion of lower-margin 
projects, or if competition intensifies in certain geographies or customer segments, gross 
margin could come under pressure. In addition, an unfavourable product mix shift towards 
less profitable work could offset benefits from backlog growth.

Overseas expansion and regulatory risks 
Acter is expanding across markets in the US, Southeast Asia, Japan and China, which 
exposes it to FX movements, local compliance requirements, tax complexity and regulatory 
uncertainty. Delays in licensing, changes in safety standards or local-content requirements 
could increase project costs and reduce execution efficiency. Any misstep in managing 
overseas operations could damage profitability and client retention. 
Key risks to our call: 
weaker-than-expected 
expansion in 
semiconductor industry, 
project delays or 
cancellations, and 
higher-than-expected 
raw-material price hikes

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Appendix 
Company background 
History 
Acter (5536 TT), founded in 1979 and headquartered in Taichung, Taiwan, is a cleanroom 
EPCM provider with over four decades of experience. Starting with early mechanical and 
electrical (M&E) engineering through vertical and horizontal integration, Acter has now 
developed into an engineering technology group with cross-border execution capabilities. A 
cleanroom building process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the 
physical plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for 
essential utility generation, where Acter is one of the experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
networks, and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. The 
company’s core competency is providing one-stop integrated total solutions, spanning 
services, primary piping networks and hook-up systems, leaving C&S services to other 
engineering peers, given the nature of the latter segment’s low margin and long timespan.

In terms of business model, Acter delivers EPCM (Engineering, Procurement, 
Construction, Management) capabilities. From initial customised planning and feasibility 
assessments to strategic procurement, installation, and post-completion maintenance, 
Acter builds production environments tailored to client process requirements.

Acter: EPCM solutions offered

Source: Company, Daiwa

Regarding global expansion, Acter actively responds to the global supply chain’s “N+1” 
strategy, operating across 11 regions with 33 companies globally. Its service network spans 
Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia (including Thailand, Malaysia, Vietnam, Indonesia, and 
Singapore), Japan, India, and the US. The company leverages a group synergy strategy 
by integrating resources from key subsidiaries – such as Novatech (6613 TT, not rated; 
provides chemical primary and hook-up systems), Rayzher (7703 TT, not rated; provides 
special gas primary and hook-up supply systems), and Acter Group (603163 CH, not rated; 
provides cleanroom engineering in China) – to form a complete technical ecosystem, 
ensuring cross-border capability and quality.

Comprehensive 
cleanroom EPCM 
provider in Taiwan; 
dominates both high-
tech market and 
consumer industry

### Page 20

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acter: global footprint

Source: Company, Daiwa

In 2Q26, Acter classified revenue as: cleanroom electromechanical (63%), water, gas and 
chemical delivery systems (28%), consumer industry electromechanical (6%), biomedical 
(2%) and high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (1%). In 2025, the product mix 
was: cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas and chemical delivery systems 
(20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and high-tech 
equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

Acter serves a diversified client base divided into high-tech and consumer divisions. The 
high-tech division focuses on advanced sectors including semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, AI 
data centres (for CSPs), and electronics assembly. The consumer division extends into 
healthcare, premium commercial properties, logistics facilities, and green engineering. This 
multi-industry strategy allows the company to capitalise on tech sector upgrades while 
maintaining resilient operations across technology (especially semiconductor) cycles.

Acter adopts a management strategy involving local hiring in overseas markets, prioritising 
collaboration with local supply chains and compliance with local regulations to reduce 
cross-border deployment costs and thus build strong local teams. Acter has more than 
2,600 employees worldwide (average age: 42 years; average length of service: 7 years). 
With more than 45 years of experience in EPCM, the company plays a reliable role for both 
high-tech and consumer industry.

saving construction methods, digital twins, ice-storage energy conservation, wastewater 
recycling, and AI smart facility management systems to help clients achieve carbon 
neutrality while expanding capacity. Backed by solid corporate governance, Acter

Glossary of terms 
EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management): Cleanroom 
construction services that cover the whole process including project design, purchasing, 
construction and management.

BIM (building information modelling): A digital modelling system of a building and its key 
information used in cleanroom construction.

C&S (civil & structural): Site infrastructure and the design and construction of load-
bearing elements.

MEP (mechanical, electrical, and plumbing): Building essential systems for environment 
control, power, lighting, water, and drainage.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 7 Oct 2026 
Source:  Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Acter maintains a structured governance framework that supports disciplined management. The
chairman is Chin-Li Liang. The company operates with defined responsibility across the board,
management, cyber security oversight, operations, and audit functions. It has established an
organisation function led by the President, a Chief Cyber Security Officer, a Response Team, an
Operations and Policy Enforcement Team, and an Audit Team to supervise policy execution and
review security implementation.
Capital management 1
Acter manages capital in a stable and shareholder-friendly manner. Over the past several years,
Acter has typically maintained a dividend payout ratio of 65-80%. It also maintains a strong
financing network across multiple banks, supporting flexibility for project execution and
expansion while preserving financial stability.
Related party &
transaction 1
Acter’s business is supported by a transparent network of suppliers, project partners, and
financial counterparties, including companies with equity ties and reciprocal business
relationships that are common in its operating ecosystem. These relationships are managed
through formal internal procedures for fund lending, endorsements and guarantees, and asset
acquisition and disposal, all of which are approved through shareholder mechanisms and
company rules. Such a structure helps keep inter-company and supply-chain transactions
organised and traceable, reducing the risk of unclear dealings.
E Product quality &safety 1
Acter executes its projects through a complete, disciplined process covering bidding
assessment, project planning, design, procurement, construction management, verification and
testing, completion and acceptance, closing review, and warranty service. Its cleanroom and
electromechanical engineering business is built around maintaining temperature, humidity, and
dust-free environments that support high-precision manufacturing. The company strengthens
quality control through BIM, Revit, simulation software, ISO drawing output tools, and automated
modeling, which improve design accuracy, construction efficiency, and project consistency.
E Ecological impacts 1
Acter has built a concrete sustainability programme with measurable environmental outcomes.
The company completed Scope 1, 2, and 3 greenhouse gas emissions calculation, obtained
third-party verification under ISO 14064-1:2018, and publicly disclosed energy consumption and
emissions data. It also implemented a strong waste reduction programme, achieving a 95%
wastepaper recycling rate at headquarters in 2025 and 100% paper recycling at the 19F and 20F
floors after establishing paper segregation and reuse procedures. The company also promoted
sustainability measures through emissions inventory management, environmental disclosures,
and resource-efficiency practices. These actions show active environmental management and a
clear commitment to lowering operational impact.
Health & safety
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

^New 
Earnings above all 
• Higher oil prices are in a tussle with stronger growth. We expect strong growth 
to help the global economy weather inflation risks, allowing equities to focus on the 
strength of earnings growth. Oil prices remain the key risk to monitor 
• Maintain a preference for equities over bonds and cash.  Strong earnings 
growth means we would use the recent pause to add exposure to our preferred 
markets and themes. The US and Asia ex-Japan remain our preferred regions 
• Take advantage of higher bond yields via credit risk but cap duration. The 
risk of higher bond yields looks most pressing in ultra -long maturities, but credit 
quality remains strong. We are Overweight EM USD bonds 
Hits from the 1990s 
Rising bond yields have been a key market theme.  Since mid -year, 10 -year 
government bond yields have risen approximately 40-80bps across the US, Japan and 
Euro area. Against this backdrop, gold has delivered high single -digit returns, while 
global equities have delivered low single-digit returns. 
Higher oil prices are in a tussle with robust growth data . The lack of progress 
towards a negotiated settlement in the US -Iran conflict and rising fears of shipping 
restrictions extending to the Bab al-Mandab Strait have meant that oil prices are once 
again testing the USD 100/bbl level. These worries have been exacerbated by 
increasing worries about refined product (eg. diesel, jet fuel, etc) supply. Despite these 
concerns, US growth data has held up remarkably well, as illustrated by the latest PMI 
and retail sales data. This is underpinned by AI investment growth.  
Real, rather than nominal, energy prices are key.  A chart of inflation -adjusted 
energy prices, though, illustrates why the global economy has managed to hold up 
despite these risks. Oil prices in real (inflation-adjusted) terms are closer to long term 
averages rather than at true ‘oil shock’ levels. This, together with the global economy’s 
lower oil intensity relative to history, is why we expect  growth and inflation to avoid 
worst-case scenarios as long as nominal oil prices avoid an excursion into the USD 
120-150/bbl range.
